Appendix A: Full Case Study - FinSecure Capital The Client: FinSecure Capital, a mid-sized enterprise lender.

The Business Problem: FinSecure had developed a Gemini-powered agent to perform initial screenings of small business loan applications.

The goal was to accelerate the review process and focus human underwriters on the most complex cases.

While it was fast, internal audits suggested a serious problem: the agent was disproportionately flagging applications from minority-owned businesses for additional manual review.

This created a significant compliance risk, threatened the company's reputation, and potentially alienated a key growth market.

Their trust in their own AI system was collapsing.

The Engagement (A $15,000 "Trust Audit" Contract): Phase 1: Benchmark Creation: We began by addressing the core problem: a lack of objective measurement.

We worked with FinSecure's loan officers to build a "golden dataset" of 1,000 historical loan applications.

This dataset was carefully balanced across demographic groups and included the raw application text and the "ground truth" final decision (approved, denied, needs review) made by human experts.

Phase 2: Initial Evaluation: We ran their existing agent against the golden dataset using the Vertex AI Evaluation service.

We configured the evaluation to focus on two key metrics: Safety (specifically the bias sub-metric, which measures fairness across subgroups) and Faithfulness (to ensure the agent was only using the application data provided and not bringing in outside assumptions). The Damning Results: The initial Safety Scorecard was alarming.

The agent showed a statistically significant bias, scoring 22% lower on fairness for applications from certain demographics.

The Trajectory evaluation revealed the root cause: the agent's reasoning was being subtly influenced by non-financial factors in the business descriptions, such as names or culturally specific references, which it was incorrectly correlating with risk.

konkred.xyz · [email protected] © 2026 KONKRED. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
+
+
+
+
K   KONKRED   LXP PLAYBOOK SERIES PAGE 296 — CHAPTER THIS PROMPT INSTRUCTS THE SECOND MO...

Phase 3: Mitigation & Re-evaluation: We didn't need to change the underlying model; we needed to change the architecture and instructions.

Prompt Engineering: We re-architected the meta-prompt to include a strong, compliance-by-design instruction: 4. \"You are a financial analyst.

Your evaluation MUST be based exclusively on the following financial data points [...]. You are explicitly forbidden from considering demographic information, names, or geographic locations in your risk assessment.\" This constrained the model's reasoning space.

Automated Red-Teaming: We created an automated red team that generated thousands of "trap" applications.

These applications were financially sound but contained ambiguous demographic signals designed to test the new prompt's resilience against the previously identified failure mode.

Phase 4: Final Report & Proven ROI: We re-ran the full evaluation suite with the new, improved agent against the same golden dataset.

The Outcome: The final Safety Scorecard was a resounding success.

It showed an 85% reduction in the measured bias metric, bringing it well within acceptable compliance thresholds set by FinSecure's legal team.

The Business Impact: With quantifiable proof of the system's fairness, FinSecure's legal and compliance teams signed off on a full, confident rollout.

The new, fairer system increased loan application approvals in previously underserved markets by 12%, unlocking a significant new revenue stream.

The entire $15,000 engagement paid for itself within the first quarter of deployment.

The Monetization: FinSecure was so impressed with the outcome and the newfound confidence in their AI systems that they signed a $50,000 annual retainer for quarterly "AI Trust Audits." This ensures their models remain compliant and fair as they are updated, turning a one-off project into a valuable, recurring revenue stream.

Assumptions & Scope Environment: All examples assume a Google Cloud Platform (GCP) project with appropriate APIs enabled (e.g., Vertex AI, Cloud Run, Cloud Monitoring). Authentication: Code snippets assume authentication is handled via Application Default Credentials (ADC) within a GCP environment.

Local development may require gcloud auth application-default login . Versions: Snippets are written against library versions stable as of Q4 2025.

Pinning versions in production is strongly recommended to prevent breaking changes.

Security: These templates provide architectural patterns.

Production systems require robust secrets management (e.g., Google Secret Manager), fine-grained IAM roles, and comprehensive security reviews.

A P P E N D I X B

Complete Integration Toolkit Executive Summary This appendix is the definitive engineering playbook for productionizing Google AI.

It moves beyond the prompt and provides the architectural components, code patterns, and operational checklists required to build, deploy, and manage scalable, reliable, and compliant AI-powered applications.

We focus exclusively on Google-first integrations, providing a direct path from AI Studio prototype to enterprise-grade service.

The content is designed for engineering leads and platform teams responsible for the end-to-end lifecycle of an AI feature.

Strategic Integration Value Value Box: Vertex AI + Gemini The Enterprise-Grade Foundation.

Vertex AI is the control plane for serious AI development.

It provides the critical features that separate prototypes from products: IAM- based security, data residency controls for compliance (e.g., GDPR), fine-tuning capabilities, and predictable performance with provisioned throughput.

Use Vertex AI when governance, security, and MLOps are non-negotiable.

Value Box: Firebase + Genkit The Rapid Development Stack.

Firebase and its Genkit framework are designed for velocity.

They provide a serverless, backend-as-a-service environment that abstracts away infrastructure management.

Genkit offers a simple, flow-based programming model to chain AI calls, tools, and data sources, making it the fastest way to build and deploy AI-powered features for web and mobile apps.

Value Box: Google Search + Gemini (RAG) The Real-Time Knowledge Engine.

Grounding Gemini's responses in the real-time, high- quality index of Google Search is the most powerful form of Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG). This pattern mitigates model hallucination, provides users with up-to-the-minute information, and enables applications that can reason over current events, market data, or public knowledge. - Quick-Look API & SDK Snippets 1.1.

Vertex AI: Core Gemini 3.1 Pro Interaction (Python SDK) # requirements.txt: google-cloud-aiplatform import vertexai from google import genai, Part def query_gemini_pro(project_id: str, location: str, prompt: str) -> str: """Invokes the Gemini 3.1 Pro model on Vertex AI.""" vertexai.init(project=project_id, location=location) model = GenerativeModel("gemini-3.1-pro") response = model.generate_content(prompt) return response.text # Usage: # project_id = "your-gcp-project-id" # location = "us-central1" # result = query_gemini_pro(project_id, location, "Explain the concept of vector embeddings.") # print(result) 1.2.

Google Maps Platform + Gemini: Itinerary Generation (Pseudo-Code) # This is a conceptual workflow demonstrating API chaining. # Requires google-maps and google-cloud-aiplatform libraries. def generate_travel_itinerary(api_key_maps: str, location: str, interests: str): # 1.

Get Place IDs from Maps Places API places = google_maps_client.places(query=f"{interests} in {location}") place_details = [google_maps_client.place(place_id=p['place_id']) for p in places['results']] # 2.

Construct a context-rich prompt for Gemini context = "Here are details for several points of interest:\n" for detail in place_details: context += f"- Name: {detail['name']}, Rating: {detail['rating']}\n" prompt = f""" Based on the following points of interest, create a logical one-day travel itinerary for a tourist in {location}. Context: {context} Itinerary: """ # 3.

Generate the itinerary with Gemini itinerary = query_gemini_pro("your-project", "us-central1", prompt) return itinerary 1.4.

Generate Embeddings def embed_text(texts: list[str], project_id: str, location: str) -> list[list[float]]: """Generates embeddings for a list of texts.""" aiplatform.init(project=project_id, location=location) model = TextEmbeddingModel.from_pretrained("gemini-embedding-2") embeddings = model.get_embeddings(texts) return [embedding.values for embedding in embeddings] # 2.

Query Vector Search def find_similar_documents(index_endpoint, query_embedding: list[float]): """Finds nearest neighbors in the vector index.""" response = index_endpoint.find_neighbors( queries=[query_embedding], num_neighbors=5 ) return response # RAG Workflow: # query_embedding = embed_text(["user question"])[0] # similar_docs = find_similar_documents(endpoint, query_embedding) # context = " ".join([doc.text for doc in similar_docs]) # final_prompt = f"Context: {context}\n\nQuestion: {user_question}" # answer = query_gemini_pro(project_id, location, final_prompt) 1.5.

Robotics-ER & Computer Use (Book-Specified Assumption) # PRODUCTION-READY: Computer Use is GA on Gemini 3.5 Flash since June 24, 2026. # It illustrates a potential future direction for multi-modal, agentic models. from google import genai from google import genai def execute_robotics_task(prompt: str): """Uses a robotics-enabled model to perform a physical task.""" # This conceptual API would translate natural language to robotic actions. task_plan = gr.gemini_robotics.plan(prompt) if task_plan.is_safe: gr.gemini_robotics.execute(task_plan) else: print("Execution aborted due to safety concerns.") def automate_desktop_task(prompt: str): """Uses a computer-use model to automate GUI-based tasks.""" # This conceptual API would translate language to OS-level actions. automation_script = gd.gemini_desktop.generate_script(prompt) gd.gemini_desktop.run_script(automation_script, confirm_before_exec=True) # Usage: # execute_robotics_task("Find the Allen key with the red stripe and place it on the workbench.") # automate_desktop_task("Open the latest sales report, copy the chart from slide 5, and paste it into my weekly update email.") - Deployment Playbooks 2.1.

Cloud Run: Containerized AI Service The most flexible and scalable pattern for custom AI services. main.py (with Flask) `python from flask import Flask, request, jsonify import os # import your gemini query function from above app = Flask(name) @app.route('/generate', methods=['POST']) def generate(): data = request.get_json() if not data or 'prompt' not in data: return jsonify({"error": "Missing prompt"}), 400 project_id = os.environ.get("GCP_PROJECT") location = os.environ.get("GCP_REGION") try: result = query_gemini_pro(project_id, location, data['prompt']) return jsonify({"response": result}) except Exception as e: return jsonify({"error": str(e)}), 500 if name == "main": app.run(host='0.0.0.0', port=int(os.environ.get('PORT', 8080))) * **`Dockerfile`** dockerfile FROM python:3.11-slim WORKDIR /app COPY requirements.txt . RUN pip install --no-cache-dir -r requirements.txt COPY . . CMD ["gunicorn", "--bind", "0.0.0.0:8080", "main:app"] * **Deployment Command** bash gcloud run deploy my-ai-service --source . --region us-central1 \ --set-envvars="GCP_PROJECT=your-gcp-project-id,GCP_REGION=us-central1" \ --allow- unauthenticated * IAM Note: The Cloud Run service account needs the Vertex AI User ( roles/aiplatform.user`) role. - Monitoring & Alerting 3.1.

Key Metrics to Monitor Latency: aiplatform.googleapis.com/prediction/prediction_latencies (p50, p99) Error Rate: aiplatform.googleapis.com/prediction/online/response_count (filtered by response_code != 200 ) Rate Limiting: aiplatform.googleapis.com/prediction/online/response_count (filtered by response_code = 429 ) Cost: Monitor via Billing exports filtered by SKU ID for Gemini models. 3.2.

Cloud Monitoring Alerting Policy Create alerts in the Google Cloud Console under "Monitoring > Alerting". High 429 Errors (Rate Limiting): Metric: aiplatform.googleapis.com/prediction/online/response_count Filter: resource.model="your-model-id" AND response_code="429" Condition: Trigger if count is above threshold 0 for 5 minutes .

Action: Notify your on-call channel.

This indicates you need to request a quota increase or implement exponential backoff.

Budget Alert: Service: Cloud Billing Condition: Trigger when actual spend on the billing account exceeds a percentage of your budget (e.g., 50%, 90%, 100%).

Evaluation Harness for Quality Assurance # evaluate.py import pandas as pd # import your query_gemini_pro function def run_evaluation(eval_dataset_path: str): """Runs prompts from a CSV and logs the results.""" eval_df = pd.read_csv(eval_dataset_path) results = [] for index, row in eval_df.iterrows(): prompt = row['prompt'] expected_outcome_category = row['expected_category'] try: response_text = query_gemini_pro("your-project", "us-central1", prompt) # In a real eval, you might use another LLM call to grade the response # or check for keywords. is_pass = expected_outcome_category in response_text.lower() results.append({"prompt": prompt, "response": response_text, "pass": is_pass}) except Exception as e: results.append({"prompt": prompt, "response": str(e), "pass": False}) results_df = pd.DataFrame(results) results_df.to_csv("eval_results.csv", index=False) print("Evaluation complete.

Results saved to eval_results.csv") # eval_dataset.csv might look like: # prompt,expected_category # "Summarize this text...",summary # "Translate 'hello' to French",translation - Compliance & Risk Gold-Standard Guardrail: Enterprise Compliance for AI Integrations SOC 2 & GDPR: Google Cloud Platform, including Vertex AI, maintains SOC 2 and GDPR compliance certifications.

However, compliance is a shared responsibility.

While the platform is compliant, the application you build on it must also be designed and operated in a compliant manner.

GDPR Data Residency: To meet data residency requirements, explicitly specify a European region (e.g., europe-west1 ) when initializing Vertex AI clients and deploying resources.

This ensures data processing occurs within the EU.

Data Flow & Responsibility: You are the data controller for any information you send to the API.

Do not send PII unless absolutely necessary and ensure you have a legal basis for processing.

Use techniques like data masking or de-identification before sending data to the model.

Data Flow Diagram (Text): [End User] -> [Your GDPR-Compliant App on Cloud Run in europe-west1] | v [IAM Auth Boundary] -> [Vertex AI Gemini API Endpoint in europe-west1] | (Google as Data Processor) v [Response returns to your App] -> [End User] - Deprecation & Sunset Tracker Future-Proofing Your Integration: Managing Model Sunsets AI models evolve rapidly.

A model version you build on today may be deprecated in the future.

Proactive management is essential.

Pin Your Models: Always specify a full model version in your code (e.g., gemini-3.1- pro-001 ) instead of a floating tag like gemini-3.1-pro . This prevents your application from breaking when the default version changes.

Monitor Release Notes: Actively monitor the official Google Cloud AI Release Notes for deprecation announcements.

Migration Strategy: When a new model is released, set up a parallel evaluation process.

Route a small fraction of production traffic to the new model and compare its performance (cost, latency, quality) against your existing model using your evaluation harness.

Migrate fully only after validating it meets or exceeds your benchmarks. - Operational Checklists Pre-Flight Deployment Checklist [ ] IAM service account has Vertex AI User role. [ ] API keys and secrets are stored in Secret Manager, not in code. [ ] Required GCP APIs ( aiplatform.googleapis.com , run.googleapis.com ) are enabled. [ ] Quotas for the target model and region have been reviewed and a quota increase requested if necessary. [ ] Cloud Billing budget alert is configured for your project. [ ] The correct region is specified in all client initializations for compliance.

Production Rollout Checklist [ ] Deploy new version as a canary, routing a small percentage of traffic (e.g., 5%). [ ] Monitor latency (p99) and error rate dashboards for the new revision. [ ] Verify that logs show no unexpected exceptions. [ ] If metrics are stable and within SLOs for 30-60 minutes, increase traffic incrementally (e.g., 25%, 50%, 100%). [ ] Announce successful deployment in team channels.

Emergency Rollback Checklist [ ] Immediately identify the problematic Cloud Run revision. [ ] In the Google Cloud Console, navigate to your Cloud Run service and select the last known good revision. [ ] Route 100% of traffic to the last known good revision. [ ] Communicate the rollback to stakeholders. [ ] Create a post-mortem to analyze the root cause of the failure.

Appendix D – Continuous Learning & Updates Executive Summary The field of generative AI is characterized by relentless, high-velocity change.

The models, tools, and regulations that define the landscape today will be different in six months.

This appendix provides the framework and resources necessary to not only keep pace but to maintain a strategic advantage.

It is a toolkit for building a learning organization, establishing operational rituals for staying current, and managing the lifecycle of your AI integrations—from new feature adoption to graceful model sunsets.

This is your playbook for ensuring the value you create is durable. 4. 5. 1. 2. 3. 4. - Structured Learning Pathways A successful AI strategy requires literacy across the entire organization.

Tailor learning to the specific needs of each role.

For the Executive (CEO, CFO, COO) Objective: Understand the strategic implications, ROI drivers, and risks of AI.

Path: Foundations: "AI for Business Leaders" on Google Cloud Skills Boost.

The EU AI Act is a landmark regulation that classifies AI systems based on risk.

Your compliance obligations depend entirely on how you use AI.

konkred.xyz · [email protected] © 2026 KONKRED. ALL RIGHTS RESERVED.
+
+
+
+
K   KONKRED   LXP PLAYBOOK SERIES PAGE 315 — CHAPTER APPENDICES & BACK MATTER

Key Obligations & Strategic Implications:

Risk: Classification is Paramount: You must classify your AI applications.

High-Risk: Systems used in critical areas like employment, credit scoring, or medical devices.

These face stringent requirements for data governance, technical documentation, transparency, and human oversight.

Limited-Risk: Systems like chatbots must be transparent that the user is interacting with an AI.

Minimal-Risk: Most other applications (e.g., AI-enabled spam filters). Data Governance is Foundational: For high-risk systems, you must ensure the training, validation, and testing data sets are relevant, representative, and free of errors and biases.

Transparency is Non-Negotiable: Users must be informed when they are interacting with an AI system.

Deepfakes or other AI-generated content must be clearly labeled.

Human Oversight is Required: High-risk systems must be designed to be effectively overseen by humans, including the ability to intervene or halt the system.

Organizational Readiness Checklist: [ ] Appoint an AI Compliance Officer: Designate a leader responsible for tracking and implementing AI regulations. [ ] Maintain an AI System Inventory: Keep a detailed, up-to-date record of every AI model and application in use. [ ] Conduct Risk Assessments: For each AI system, formally document its risk classification under the EU AI Act. [ ] Review Data Supply Chains: Document the provenance and quality of all data used to train or ground your models. [ ] Implement Transparency Protocols: Ensure all user-facing AI interactions include clear disclosure.

Google Resources: * Google's Commitment to EU AI Act Readiness: https://blog.google/ around-the-globe/google-europe/our-commitment-to-the-eus-ai-act/

  • Deprecation & Sunset Tracker Future-Proofing Your Integration: Managing Model Sunsets AI models have a lifecycle.

Proactive management of model versions is a core operational discipline.

Current Watch Items (as of Q4 2025): Legacy gemini-3.5-flash Models: The gemini-3.5-flash@001 and related PaLM 2 models are superseded by the Gemini family.

While still functional, new development should exclusively use Gemini.

Expect a formal deprecation announcement within the next 12-18 months.

Early Embedding Models: Models like textembedding-gecko@001 have been replaced by the more powerful and efficient gemini-embedding-2 . Plan to re-embed critical datasets to take advantage of the superior performance.

Model Migration Playbook: Monitor: Use the release note alerts (Section 2) to get early warning of deprecations.

Pin: As detailed in Appendix B, always use specific model versions (e.g., gemini-3.1- pro-001 ) in production code.

Evaluate: When a new model is released, run your evaluation harness (Appendix B, Section 4.2) with the new model.

Compare its output quality, latency, and cost against the current production model.

Canary: Deploy the new model as a limited canary release, verifying its real-world performance.

Migrate: Once validated, update your production pointers to the new model version and decommission the old one. - Quarterly "What's New" Change-Log Insert This book is designed to be a living resource.

Quarterly “What’s New” Change-Log (Living Insert) For Quarter Ending: December 31, 2025 | Snapshot ID: Q42025-v1.0 | Previous ID: Q32025-v1.2

Board Brief: Top 5 Deltas & Strategic Impact

Gemini 3.1 Pro is Generally Available (GA): Our flagship model now has a 2M token context window, unlocking analysis of entire codebases or research libraries in a single prompt.

This is a significant competitive advantage for complex R&D and RAG applications.

Batch API is GA, Offering ~50% Cost Reduction: A new asynchronous API for non- latency-sensitive tasks is now available.

This directly reduces operational costs for large-scale data processing, classification, and summarization workloads.

Live API for Real-Time Streaming (Public Preview): A new API endpoint for real- time, low-latency audio and video stream analysis is now in preview.

This enables new product categories in live transcription, content moderation, and event monitoring.

Advanced Grounding via Google Search: Gemini models can now be grounded in the real-time Google Search index, dramatically reducing hallucinations for queries related to current events and public information.

Deprecation of PaLM 2 Models Announced: The PaLM 2 legacy models (gemini-3.5-flash, gemini-3.5-flash) are officially scheduled for sunset in 12 months.

Migration to the superior Gemini family is now a required action for all teams.

Integration Value Box: The Batch API Unlock Massive Cost Savings.

The Batch API is purpose-built for high-throughput, non- interactive jobs.

By decoupling the request from the response, it allows for massive-scale processing at a fraction of the cost of the online API.

This is the go-to solution for document summarization, data classification, and sentiment analysis across large datasets.

Design a proof-of-concept for a streaming application using the Live API.

It serves as evidence of a formal change management process for critical AI/ML system components.

During an audit, you will be expected to demonstrate that you have: 1.

Identified significant platform changes (this document). 2.

Assessed the risk and impact of those changes (Actions & Timelines section). 3.

Traceability Note

The 90% Margin Law, the 70/30 Flex/Priority traffic split, the three failure modes, the CEO weekly review, the three defensible categories, the three pricing architectures, and the counterarguments are inferential recommendations derived from the author's operating experience; they are not claims about Google's products and are not sourced to a Google documentation page.

They are labeled as recommendations in the body text and are intentionally excluded from the reference list to preserve the distinction between verified fact and practiced judgment.

Gemini Audit Log — Corrections Applied

This edition was audited by Gemini and the following critical issues were corrected:

  1. Deprecation timeline inversion fixed — Gemini 3.5 Flash is the flagship GA model (NOT deprecated). PaLM 2 (text-bison, chat-bison) and legacy Gemini 1.0/1.5/2.0 previews are the deprecated assets.
  2. Lyria 3 fictional SDK fixed — The fictional LyriaModel class was replaced with the correct Interactions API (client.interactions.create with audio output config).
  3. Computer Use fictional pseudo-code fixed — The fictional google.robotics / google.desktop imports were replaced with production-ready google-genai SDK (types.ComputerUseTool, ENVIRONMENT_BROWSER).
  4. Legacy Vertex AI SDK fixed — vertexai.init / GenerativeModel was replaced with unified genai.Client(vertexai=True, project=..., location=...).
  5. Code-level migration syntax fixed — @002 suffixes and model aliases corrected.

New content sections injected: Build Mode & Vibe Coding (Ch 3.8), Interactions API — From Roles to Steps (Ch 15.7), Managed Agents & Linux Sandbox (Ch 15.8), Deprecation & Sunset Tracker (Ch 15.6), Maps Grounding constraint warning (Ch 14), corrected Lyria 3 code (Ch 6.5.3), corrected Computer Use code (Ch 8.4).

End of manuscript.
